欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道 
金融市場が動揺するリスクは当面残存する見通し

○ このところ欧州ではインフレに伴う生活苦や公共サービスの劣化等を背景に各地で大規模抗議活
動が発生している 
○ 大規模デモは緊縮を伴う財政健全化を難しくする要因となる。財政懸念や政情不安は国債利回り
の上昇やユーロ安を招いており、経済への悪影響も懸念される 
○ 来年にかけて、欧州では複数の重要な国政選挙が予定される中、政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金
融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存するだろう

１．欧州で広がる大規模抗議活動 
このところ、欧州では大規模デモが多発している（図表1）。フランスでは、9月以降、教員不足や
学校施設の不備など、教育環境の改善を求める高校生の抗議活動が続いていた。こうした中で、10月
半ばには、物価高への対策や公共サービスの改善を求める市民も加わり、抗議活動は全国規模に拡大
した。 
大規模デモが発生しているのはフランスだけではない。フランスのデモに一部触発される形でベル
図表 1 9 月以降に欧州で発生した主な抗議活動 図表2 EU：生活苦の割合（左）と 
自国が抱える課題（右）

### Page 2

2

ギーやイタリア等でも抗議活動が発生している。また、スペインでは、高齢女性が家賃を支払えずに
強制退去させられたことをきっかけに、住宅不足や住宅価格・家賃高騰等に対する不満が爆発する形
で大規模な抗議活動が発生し、これが遠因となりサンチェス首相は議会の解散に追い込まれた。 
各地で発生する大規模抗議活動の背景には、近年のインフレによる生活苦やこれを受けた政府への
不満がある1。EUにおいて、生活苦を訴える人の割合は低中所得層を中心に2022年以降高まっている
ほか、自国が直面する課題としてインフレや経済状況を挙げる人の割合も高い（図表2）。これらの要
因が、自国政府に対する信頼感の低迷につながっている（図表3）。 
本稿では、生活改善を求めて各国で広がる抗議活動が経済や財政、金融市場に与える影響について
考察する。 
２．大規模抗議活動は財政健全化を阻む要因に 
一連の抗議活動は、2018年にフランスで発生した黄色いベスト運動を想起させる2。当時の参加者は、
自動車に常備が義務付けられている黄色い安全ベストを着用し、燃料税の増税や生活費高騰に抗議し
た。購買力の低下や政府への不満が背景にある点は当時と共通している。異なるのは、政府の追加的
な財政支出の余地が、当時と比べてさらに狭まっていることである。 
欧州債務危機が起きた2010年代前半以降、順調に進捗してきた政府債務の削減は、2020年代に入る
と足踏みしはじめた。パンデミック時の大規模給付やウクライナ戦争後のエネルギー高対策など、危
機対応の財政出動が頻発したためだ。黄色いベスト運動が発生した2018年と比べて政府債務が増加し
たEU加盟国は、27カ国中18カ国にのぼる（図表4）。 
加えて、足元では防衛費増額や社会保障費・利払い負担の拡大など、構造的な財政支出圧力も高ま
っている。各国の次年度予算案を踏まえると、財政赤字は当面、高止まりする公算が大きい（図表5）。
安全保障環境の変化やエネルギー価格の高騰を踏まえ、EUは一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連の追加支
出をEU財政ルールの対象外とする柔軟化措置を設けている。しかし、当該措置を適用しているドイツ
図表3 EU市民の自国政府への信頼度 図表4 EU加盟国の一般政府債務残高の変化 
（2018年～2025年）

### Page 3

3

やベルギーでは、特例対象の追加支出を除いてもなお、財政赤字をGDP比3％に抑制するEUの基準を
超過する見込みである。それでもベルギーでは、連立与党間の予算協議が決裂し、首相が辞意を表明
する事態に陥った。 
フランスを起点に広がった抗議活動は、緊縮を伴う財政健全化の難しさを浮き彫りにしている。2018
年の黄色いベスト運動では、マクロン政権が燃料税増税の撤回や所得支援策の拡充に踏み切ったこと
で、抗議活動が漸く沈静化に向かった。今回は、11月に新たなデモやストライキが計画されるなど、
いまだ抗議活動が収束する見通しは立っていない。各国政府は事態の長期化を回避するため、家計支
援や公共サービス拡充などの対応を迫られる可能性があり、財政拡張圧力が一段と強まることが懸念
される。 
３．政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存 
金融市場は、欧州主要国の財政状況に対する警戒を強めている。 
フランスをはじめとする高債務国では、政治や財政を巡る混乱を嫌気して、10年債利回りが上昇し
ている（図表6）。2010年代の欧州債務危機を受けてECB（欧州中央銀行）は、条件付きで対象国の国
債を無制限に購入可能なOMT（Outright Monetary Transactions）や経済の基礎的条件では正当化されな
い国債市場の混乱が金融政策の波及を阻害する場合に発動可能なTPI （ Transmission Protection 
Instrument）などの市場安定化措置3を整備してきた。当時よりセーフティーネットが整備されている
こともあり、足元の国債利回り上昇が欧州債務危機時のような金融危機につながる可能性は現時点で
は低い。 
とはいえ、金利の上昇は景気下押しや、さらなる財政悪化の要因となる。金利上昇幅が大きいフラ
ンスを例に足元の利回り上昇幅を踏まえて試算すると、設備投資・住宅投資が1年後に3％程度減少す
るとの結果が得られる（図表7）。金利上昇やそれに伴う景気減速は、利払い費の増加や税収の減少を
図表5 主要国の財政収支実績と見通し 図表6 ユーロ圏の高債務国と 
ドイツの10年債利回り

きなかった国。数値は成立前の政府予算案ベース。

### Page 4

4

通じて、財政をさらに悪化させる要因にもなる。 
各国の政情不安や財政への懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営をも左右する可能性がある。足元では、ユ
ーロが主要通貨に対して下落している（図表8）。ユーロ安は、輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ率を
押し上げる要因となる。足元のユーロ下落幅を前提に、時系列モデルを用いてユーロ圏のインフレ率
への影響を試算すると、総合インフレ率は0.3％Pt程度、コアインフレ率は0.1％Pt程度押し上げられる
との結果が得られた（図表9）。ユーロ圏では、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰を背
景に、直近でもインフレ率が加速している。先行きユーロ安圧力が強い状況が続けば、インフレ率に
一段の上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。しかし、ECBはインフレ圧力が一段と強まっても、状況によ
っては追加利上げに踏み切りにくくなることが想定される。利上げがさらなる長期金利の上昇、国債
市場の不安定化を招くおそれがあるためだ。このように政情不安や財政懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営
を困難にする要因にもなりかねない。 
各国の財政や政治情勢に対する金融市場の懸念は当面残存する可能性が高い。欧州では、今年11月
にスペイン議会選挙、来年にはフランス大統領選挙やイタリア議会選挙など、重要な国政選挙が相次
いで予定されている。大規模デモの発生に加えて選挙も控える中、各国政府は国民に負担を求める緊
縮的な財政政策を例年以上に打ち出しにくくなっている。また、各国の選挙では極右政党やポピュリ
スト政党の勢力拡大が予想され、選挙後の政治はより一層不安定化するリスクがある。このほか、コ
ロナ禍からの経済復興を目的に設立されたEU復興基金の受け取りが終了することも、来年以降の財政
懸念を強める要因になるだろう。同基金の配分が手厚かった南欧や中東欧諸国は、自国の財政負担を
抑えながら公共投資などを通じて経済成長を支えてきたが、来年以降はこうした効果が徐々に剥落す
ることになる。

### Page 5

5

以上のように、2020年代に入ってから、コロナ禍におけるサプライチェーン混乱、ロシアのウクラ
イナ侵攻やイラン情勢の緊迫によるエネルギー高が立て続けに起きてインフレが継続する中、欧州各
国で有権者の生活コスト上昇に対する不満が強まっている。一方、これらの緊急事態に対応するため
財政出動を繰り返してきたことに加え、高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加、地政学リスクの高まりを背
景とした防衛費増強の必要性、利払い費の増大に直面しており、各国の財政余力は著しく低下してい
る。来年にかけて予定されている主要国の選挙では、極右・ポピュリスト政党の勢力拡大が予想され
ており、政治の混乱に拍車がかかる懸念もある。欧州の政治情勢や財政状況に対する不安が来年にか
けて沈静化するとは考えにくく、国債市場や為替市場が動揺するリスクに引き続き留意する必要があ
ろう。

### Page 1

M
Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Component supply to improve: While component supply could stay tight near
term, Delta expects to secure bigger volume in the coming quarters, so it should
have better order fulfillment ahead. Oct-26 monthly sales seems to point to double-
digit% QoQ revenue growth in 4Q26, in our view, and it is likely to see better than
seasonal growth in 1Q27. It expects the component supply tightness to start easing

in 2Q27.
HVDC contribution minor  near term: It should start to ship HVDC power racks in
4Q26, but the contribution should be relatively small. For the GPU platform,
scheduled for 2H27, Delta sees 80VDC as the only reference design as of now,
although it does not seem to be finalized yet. Our conversations indicate most
investors do not have that as the base case, so there could be upside to consensus
estimates in 2028, if that ends up being the case.

Fuel cell another driver longer term: Delta will have some pilot production in late
2026. Its BoD has approved a greenfield project for the fuel cell business. This fits
well into its power delivery business, especially amid the power constraints that
hyperscalers are being confronted with now.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)

Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
WT Microelectronics Co. Ltd.

(3036.TW) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$296.00 NT$174.00
WPG Holdings (3702.TW) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$160.00 NT$79.00
Despite strong 3Q26 results and resilient near-term guidance at
both WT Microelectronics and WPG Holdings, we see visibility
risks into 2028. Memory price normalization and rising USD
funding costs prompt us to trim expectations across Greater
China semi distributors.
Peaking momentum meets rising 2027-28 headwinds: Both WT and WPG posted

record top-lines and profit scale in 3Q26 on AI server buildouts and sharp memory
price inflation; yet, we are wary of several late-cycle pressures. First, on AI
infrastructure, our US team recently shifted ~5 GW of US data center capacity from
2028 into 2029 post-US midterms (link), introducing procurement timing risk.
Second, we caution that memory may still be cyclical after all, and we may now be
late into the cycle; (Old Memory: Peak Pricing Power?; Memory – Key Debate Post

Mid-terms (Shawn Kim)).  Third, elevated memory prices are already weighing on
traditional consumer end markets. Finally, rapid revenue expansion has driven heavy
working capital outflows and debt accumulation just as our US Economics team
forecasts two 25 bps Fed hikes in Dec and Mar to 4.25%-4.50%, keeping USD
financing costs elevated (link).
WT Microelectronics (3036.TW) 3Q26 review (link): For 4Q26, management
guided revenue to reaccelerate to a midpoint of NT$750bn (+26% Q/Q, +119% Y/Y)

on high double-digit Q/Q data center growth, with GM stepping down to a midpoint
of 3.24% (-0.36ppt Q/Q) on mix. Net profit guidance was a midpoint of NT$11.5bn
(+12% Q/Q, +17% Y/Y). 4Q26 financing costs were guided up to NT$2.7bn (vs. NT
$1.94bn in 3Q26) with no timeline for operating cash flow turning positive.
WPG Holdings (3702.TW) 3Q26 review: 3Q26 revenue was NT$510bn (+11.8% Q/Q,
+108.6% Y/Y), GM was 4.42%, and net profit was NT$10.39bn (+20.5% Q/Q,

+226.9% Y/Y). Memory surged to 58% of 3Q26 revenue (from ~25% historically)
and compute (AI and server) reached 52%, while communication/mobile mix was 11%
and consumer mix was 9%. Management noted its ship-and-debit model mitigates
inventory write-down risk, and its LaaS platform grew GMV 75.6% Y/Y (revenue
+15.5% Y/Y). For 4Q26, WPG guided revenue at a midpoint of NT$505bn, -1.0% Q/Q,
GM at a midpoint of 4.40%, and EPS at a midpoint of NT$5.47. Financial cost/sales

as at ~0.8% (two-thirds USD-denominated).
Stock implications: WT: downgrading to EW, PT cut to NT$174. 2026/27/28 EPS
revised by +1%/-6%/-5%. WPG: downgrading to EW, PT cut to NT$79. 2026/27/28
EPS revised by +46%/+10%/-25%.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

WT Micro: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$174: We update our residual income model to reflect
earnings estimate revisions. We reduce our terminal growth rate assumption from 3%  to
2%, in line with longer term global macro economic growth rate. We cut our intermediate
growth rate assumption from 8.0% to 4.0% to reflect demand uncertainties in the end
market. We also raise our beta estimate from 0.90 to 1.10 to reflect potentially peaking

growth and demand uncertainties, and this leads to an increased cost of equity from 13.3%
to 15.6%. Other key RI assumptions are unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, and  cash dividend
payout ratio of 85%. We believe WT deserves a high beta due to the high liabilities to
equity ratio (5.92x as of 3Q26) and the strong correlation with the wider Taiwan stock
market due to the nature of the distribution business.
We downgrade WT to EW. We believe the risk factors we’re seeing are likely to persist

and the stock price will likely remain under pressure unless there is further clarity or
policy reversals; we do not recommend accumulation even though the stock price has
corrected significantly from its peak in May.

WPG: Estimate Revisions Summary
We revise 2026-28e EPS by +46%/+10%/-25% each:   This factors in 2-3Q26 actual results
and 4Q26 guidance, which led to stronger 2026 estimates, as well as some near-term
resilience into 2027 with sustained memory shipment volume. Further into 2027 and
2028, we factor in an increasingly uncertain investment environment and therefore reduce
our estimates for non-operating income, mainly investment income.

WPG: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$79: We update our residual income model to reflect
earnings estimate revisions. We also raise our beta estimate from 1.3 to 1.5 to reflect
potentially peaking growth and demand uncertainties, and raised our risk premium from
6% to 8% to reflect market volatilities; this leads to an increased cost of equity from 9.5%
to 13.9%. We also raise our risk free rate from 1.5% to 2.0% to reflect rising funding costs.

Our intermediate growth rate is reduced from 5.5% to 4.0% to reflect demand
uncertainties. Other key RI assumptions are unchanged: cash dividend payout ratio of
80%, and long-term growth rate of 2.0%. We believe WPG deserves a high beta due to its
high liabilities to equity ratio (5.85x as of 3Q26) and the strong correlation to the wider
Taiwan stock market due to the nature of the distribution business.
We downgrade WPG to EW. We believe the risk factors we’re seeing are likely to persist

and the stock price will likely remain under pressure unless there is further clarity or
policy reversals; we do not recommend accumulation even though the stock price has
corrected significantly from its peak in August.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

力旺(3529)：2H26 權利金大幅成長、Security 長期機會大 
建議：維持買進

一、2026 年前三季權利金營收
+13.4%YOY，PUF-based 權利
金+927%YOY 
 2026 前三季權利金營收 21.49 億元，+13.4%YOY。 
成熟製程 NeoBit 佔 24.6%，+8.6%YOY。 
先進製程 NeoFuse 佔 70.7%，+12.3%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 1.5%，+927%YOY。 
MTP 佔 3.2%，+32.5%YOY。 
權利金成長預計將持續加速，主要受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產
品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組
合比重提高。

二、2026 年前三季授權金
+38.2% YOY，PUF - based 
+234% YOY 
 2026 前三季授權金營收 12.51 億元，+38.2%YOY。 
NeoBit 佔 11.9%，-39.3%YOY。 
NeoFuse 佔 36.5%，+35.1%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 31.7%，+234%YOY。 
MTP 佔 19.9%，+22.9%YOY。 
授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬體安
全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。

三、維持買進理由  1) 受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的
營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組合比重提高，權利金成長預計將
持續加速。 
2) 授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬
體安全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。 
3) PUF-based 已貢獻 2026 年前三季營收 12.6%，+252%YOY。應用面包括
CPU、Storage、光通、FPGA、高速傳輸晶片多面向。Arm Neoverse CSS V3
已將力旺的 PUF 列入核心元件。

리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4 의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK 하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340 원에서 1,315 원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030 년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100 조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026 년 예상 FCF 의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.  
Buy (Maintain)

## Document 269: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.
Business creations by sector: a boom that is building momentum and spreading

We have calculated the year-on-year change in the number of new business start-ups. The table categorises these results as positive (red), neutral (white) or negative (blue). The darker
the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

micro-entrepreneurs, since 2000; United Kingdom = businesses registered for VAT/PAYE since 2017; United States = business applications (tax identification number) since 2004. Sectors
have been grouped together to ensure that the classification is standardised across all countries. For the third quarter, the German data only include the months of July and August.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
Article written with the help of Ece Usta (intern).

## Document 278: AI繁荣背后，美国经济正埋下一颗“资本撤离”炸弹

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI繁荣背后，美国经济正埋下一颗“资本撤离”炸弹.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美国经常账⼾⾚字对外国股权资本的依赖升⾄历史⾼位， AI 投资热潮背后隐藏着脆弱的融
资结构。彭博宏观策略师 Simon White 警告，⼀旦 AI 需求降温、科技股下跌，外国资本可
能迅速撤离，推⾼融资成本并引发投资收缩，形成冲击经济的负反馈循环。当前衰退⻛险
尚低，但潜在脆弱性正在累积。
摘要：
美国经济正站在⼀个脆弱的平衡点上。 AI 投资热潮驱动着经济增⻓，但为这场盛宴买单的，越来
越多是随时可能撤离的外国股权资本 —— ⼀旦市场情绪逆转，这⼀融资结构将成为触发衰退的
引线。
据彭博宏观策略师 Simon White 分析，美国经常账⼾⾚字⽬前⼏乎完全依赖对回报敏感的外国股
权资⾦流⼊来填补，这⼀依赖程度已创历史纪录。股权资本不同于债务融资或直接投资，其流
动性强、波动性⾼，是最容易 " 说⾛就⾛ " 的资本形式。⼀旦股价⾛弱、市场情绪转向，今⽇的资
⾦流⼊便可能迅速变为明⽇的资⾦外逃。
当前， AI 直接相关投资单独⽀撑着近⼗分之⼀的 GDP ⽔平，约四分之⼀的名义年增⻓率（ 5.9% ）

来⾃ AI 领域。然⽽，⻩仁勋突然离开英伟达，以及⺠主党在中期选举中的胜利为数据中⼼建设前
景蒙上阴影，正令市场对 AI 需求能否持续产⽣更多疑问。这些不确定性叠加美国经济对外部股权
融资的⾼度依赖，使得潜在的 " 外部融资冲击 " ⻛险正从幕后⾛向台前。
股权融资依赖创纪录，经常账⼾⾚字⾼度暴露
AI 投资规模之庞⼤，已远超美国国内储蓄所能承载的上限。
据 Simon White 分析，外国投资者正在为逾万亿美元的 AI 相关投资提供资⾦，⽽这些资⾦⼏乎全
部以购买美国股票的形式流⼊ —— 这⼀现象在美国乃⾄全球历史上均属罕⻅。
这意味着，美国经常账⼾⾚字的融资来源，已从相对稳定的债务⼯具或外商直接投资，⾼度集
中于波动性极强的股权资本。股权资本的本质决定了其脆弱性：当股价下跌、流动性收紧，资
⾦外流的速度可以极为迅猛，进⽽推⾼资本成本，并对依赖外部融资的科技投资热潮形成直接
打击。
历史上与美国当前处境最为接近的案例，是 2006 年前后的南⾮。据 IMF 数据，彼时南⾮流⼊股
权投资占 GDP 约 5% ，经常账⼾⾚字约为 GDP 的 6.5% ，股权融资覆盖率刚刚超过 75%。随后全

球⾦融危机爆发，资本⼤规模外逃，南⾮股市以美元计价跌去近三分之⼆，货币贬值约⼀半，
并引发了⼀场短暂但剧烈的衰退，百万⼈因此失业。 Simon White 指出，尽管当前并⽆银⾏业危
机迹象，但南⾮的案例清晰揭⽰了美国在股市进⼀步⾛弱或全球其他地区陷⼊衰退时所⾯临的
脆弱性。
英伟达变局与需求隐忧， AI 投资逻辑遭遇考验
⽀撑这⼀融资结构的核⼼前提，是市场对 AI 需求持续旺盛的信⼼。然⽽，这⼀前提正受到多重因
素的侵蚀。
⻩仁勋突然离开英伟达，令算⼒供需图景骤然清晰起来。英伟达此前扮演着 AI 领域 " 事实上的央
⾏ " ⻆⾊，不仅为新兴云计算公司（ neocloud ）及其他算⼒买家提供资⾦⽀持，还为芯⽚融资提
供背书，以此托举⾃⾝产品需求。 Huang 本⼈更常常亲⾃参与英伟达的商业谈判。其离职带来的

国内投资的冲击依然不可忽视。当国内投资收缩、营收转负，衰退的条件便趋于成熟。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“Australia’s labour market delivered a second
consecutive shock in October, pushing annual
employment growth well below working-age

population growth."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

### Page 7

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.
AUS: October CPI (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.4%  
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.6
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.41%mth with the annual 
pace lifting from 4.3%yr to 4.8%yr, the fastest pace in the 
short history. Westpac expects this to be the peak in current 
acceleration in annual inflation. Our seasonally adjusted CPI 
estimate lifts 0.7%mth, a small step up from the 0.6% increase 
in September, highighting the seasonal softness in the month. 
Transport makes the largest contribution (+0.35ppts), almost 
entirely due to auto fuel, with smaller contributions from 
clothing & footwear (+0.06ppts), household goods & services 
(+0.05ppts) and meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The 
main offsets are recreation & culture (–0.18ppts) with fruit & 
vegetables, utilities and others providing marginal offsets.
For more details please see “October CPI Preview“.  
AUS: October Trimmed Mean (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.4% 
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.5
Our October Trimmed Mean estimate is 0.42%mth, a step up from 
0.3% in September, taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr 
and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 4.0%yr, 
which is also the fastest pace in the short history of the series. 
Rents and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
We see risks as slightly tilted to the upside. Our estimates 
allow for a greater degree of price resetting in areas such as 
meals out & takeaway and personal and household services, 
but this adjustment proves broader than our assumptions. 
Fuel levy surcharges are another source of upside risk, 
including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post. The 
key downside risk remains housing, particularly new dwelling 
prices, which surprised to the downside in recent months.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 retail spending (%qtr)
Nov 23, Last: -0.5, Westpac: 1.4
The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high
fuel prices, core spending (excl. fuels and vehicles) remained

resilient, rising by 0.7%. With fuel prices dropping through
much of the September quarter, we expect overall spending
levels will lift by 1.4%. Importantly, with lower fuel prices
putting money back into households’ pockets, that’s likely to
be underpinned by firm gains in core spending categories.
AUS: Q3 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Nov 26, Last: –3.6%, WBC f/c: 10.0%
Mkt f/c: 4.5%, Range: 2.0% to 10.0%
Private capex declined 3.6%qtr in Q2 2026 as data centre-

related machinery and equipment investment pulled back
sharply. With nominal ADP equipment imports surging
83%qtr to another record high, we expect a sizeable rebound
in the Q3 figures. Buildings and structures should also find
support as the broader strength in private non-residential
approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

## Document 311: Claude证明黎曼假设“大反转”：论证无效，只是空转？

Source PDF: all report pdf/Claude证明黎曼假设“大反转”：论证无效，只是空转？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

11 ⽉ 20 ⽇， Anthropic 数学家 Alpöge 宣称与 Claude 证明黎曼假设，公开 1700 万⾏ Lean 代
码。但数学家 Nielsen 同⽇发⽂指出， Alpöge“ 发布的内容不包含黎曼假设的证明。所提证
明⽆效 ” 。她认为，这个证明 “ 形式通过、实质为空 ” 。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇， Anthropic 数学家 Levent Alpöge 在个⼈⽹站发布说明，宣布⾃⼰与 Claude 共同找到
了黎曼假设的证明。
他没有交出⼈类可读的论⽂，⽽是直接放出⼀个 Lean 4 代码库： 175,187 个⽂件、约 1700 万⾏
代码。黎曼假设 1859 年由 Bernhard Riemann 提出，是克雷数学研究所悬赏 100 万美元的千禧年
问题之⼀，⾄今已悬置约 167 年。
次⽇，Lean Focused Research Organization 与 Mathlib Initiative 联合表⽰： “ 我们有充分信
⼼认为， Alpöge 关于黎曼假设的证明是正确的。 ”据《卫报》报道，两家机构是在审阅其 Lean 代

码后作出这⼀表态的。 MIT 数学家 Andrew Sutherland 也独⽴编译了该结果，他说： “ 这确实是⼀
个历史性的时刻，世界已经改变。 ”
然⽽就在同⼀天，另⼀份⽂稿出现了。
数学家 J. L. Nielsen （署名单位为堪萨斯州 Center for Topological Physics ）于 11 ⽉ 20 ⽇发布论
⽂《 Alpöge 所提黎曼假设证明的⽆效性》。她的判断⾮常直接： “ 发布内容不包含黎曼假设的证
明。所提证明⽆效。 ”
反转：⼏何条件是 “ 永远为真 ”
Nielsen 检查的是那份 175,187 个⽂件的 Lean 归档，逐条追踪顶层定理的证明项依赖。她认为
Lean 算对了，但算的东西是空的。
Lean 是 “ 证明助⼿ ” ，它不判断⼀个数学想法⾼不⾼级，只检查⼀件事：从你写下的定义出发，能
不能⼀步步推到结论。
Nielsen 翻查源码后发现，证明路径中需要⼀类 “ ⼏何对象 ” 作为⻅证（ witness ，即⽤来⽀撑某
个论断的具体例⼦）。但代码⾥这个条件是这样写的：

anyGeometry : GeoP := fun _ _ _ => True
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

翻译成⼈话就是：不管输⼊什么，⼀律返回 “ 成⽴ ” 。
第⼀处是⼏何条件。代码中有⼀个名为anyGeometry的谓词，定义为fun _ _ _ => True
—— ⽆论输⼊什么，它都返回 “ 真 ” 。按论⽂的说法，这个定义在 24 个⽂件中被使⽤了 118 次，其
中包括最终证明链上的关键 “ ⻅证 ” （ witness ，即论证中需要举出的具体对象）。
第⼆处是⼏何对象本⾝。代码⽤⼀个名为geoDegenerate的 “ 退化模型 ” 充当⼏何⻅证：体积为
0 、范数为 0 、曲线退化成⼀个点、曲⾯类型为空集。所有正性条件（如 nef 、 big ）都被定义为
True。论⽂写道，这个模型 “ 只携带⼀个旗流形的度数数字，背后没有任何簇 ” 。
第三处是⾼度函数。证明需要某个⼏何对象具有⼀个 “ ⾼度 ” （ height ，可粗略理解为衡量对象复杂
程度的数值），并被夹在上下界之间。但代码⾥，这个⾼度先被设为 0 ，随后被直接改写成⼀个
显式公式hExpl6，于是 “ ⾼度 = 公式 ” 这⼀步由rfl（⾃反性，即 “ 因为定义如此 ” ）⾃动成⽴。论

⽂的原话是：⾼度 “ 不是从任何⼏何对象推导出来的，⽽是⼀个公式，被选中以让论证闭合 ” 。
Nielsen 认为，⼏何侧与解析侧被这三步强制成了同⼀个对象，本该 “ 夹住 ” 独⽴⼏何⾼度的不等
式，实际是 “ ⼀个量和它⾃⼰⽐较 ” 。
Nielsen 的结论是：这些条件属于 “ 空真（ vacuously true ） ”—— 她写道： “ 形式化所证明的只是
同 义 反 复 —— 黎 曼 假 设 等 于 黎 曼 假 设 。 ” 即 形 式 上 为 真 ， 但 没 有 断 ⾔ 任 何 实 质 内 容 。 （ The
proposed proof is invalid: what has been checked is a term whose structural witnesses are
vacuously true, not the argument those witnesses stand for. ）。
所提出的证明是⽆效的：所检验的是⼀个结构性证据空洞成⽴的术语，⽽不是这些证据所代表的

论点。

### Page 3

⽀持者：内核通过就是通过
另⼀边的声⾳同样响亮。
Lean 与 Mathlib 社 区 的 验 证 针 对 的 是 另 ⼀ 组 问 题 ： 顶 层 定 理 是 否 匹 配 Mathlib 中 标 准 的
RiemannHypothesis定义、代码能否编译、多个独⽴检查器是否接受。这些检查全部通过，
且代码中没有axiom（额外公理）、没有sorry（占位符）、没有native_decide（绕过内核的
判定）。
在他们看来，这已经⾜够：⼀个类型正确的封闭项，就是该陈述的证明。
Polymarket 在 11 ⽉ 22 ⽇发帖概括了这场争议： “ 新分析声称 AI 辅助的黎曼假设证明⽆效，尽管它
已在 Lean 中成功验证。 ” 这条帖⼦获得 76.8 万次浏览，评论区迅速分裂。
⼤量反驳指向 Nielsen 本⼈。有⽤⼾翻出她的 PhilPapers 记录，称她此前对 OpenAI 的 Yang-
Mills 证明、 Navier-Stokes 证明都发过类似 “ ⽆效 ” 论⽂；也有⼈指出 “Center for Topological

Physics” 的出版物列表上只有她⼀⼈。⼀条⾼赞评论写道： “Lean 说有效，⼀位数学家说⽆效，
你赌谁？ ”

### Page 4

也有技术性反驳。有⽤⼾称，尼尔森 “ 犯了⼀个新⼿错误：以为恒真的前提可以平凡地推出黎曼
假设 ”—— ⾔下之意，恒真前提只能让那⼀步⽆内容，不能⾃动让最终结论失效。

### Page 5

争议的核⼼：定义算不算数学
这场争论真正卡住的地⽅，是形式验证的两个层次被混在了⼀起。
第⼀层：在给定定义和公理下，代码是否导出了⽬标定理？ Lean 社区的回答是肯定的。
第⼆层：这些定义是否忠实对应了研究者想表达的数学内容？ Nielsen 的回答是否定的。
⽤⼤⽩话说： Lean 能保证 “ 从你写下的规则出发，推理没有⼀步⾛错 ” ，但不能保证 “ 你写下的规
则就是你想说的那件事 ” 。如果规则⾥埋了⼀句 “ ⼏何条件永远成⽴ ” ，那么依赖它的推理再严
密，也没有真正触及⼏何。
有媒体的报道概括称：争议 “ 并⾮单纯指控 Lean 编译器出错，⽽是针对证明中部分数学对象的定
义提出异议 ” ；同时强调， Nielsen 的论⽂ “ 仍属可接受公开检验与反驳的研究主张，⽽⾮数学界
已取得共识的最终判决 ” 。
⼈类可读版本的缺席
还有⼀个细节让争论难以收场：没有论⽂。
传统数学证明，同⾏可以逐⾏阅读、质疑、重写。⽽这次，外界⾯对的是⼀座 1700 万⾏的代码
库。 Nielsen 在论⽂中说明，她读了约 70 个⽂件，多数是全⽂，聚焦顶层⽂件与第 1 ⾄ 7 节点名的

定义和引理 —— 相对于 175,187 个⽂件，这是⼀个极⼩的采样。
这意味着双⽅其实都还没有完成 “ ⼈类层⾯的消化 ” 。 MIT 数学家 Andrew Sutherland 在验证通过
当天就说： “ 要理解证明的数学细节，还有很多⼯作要做。我迫不及待想钻研进去。 ”

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

► 輝達2027財年第三季財報(營收、EPS、數據
中心)超預期，營收較去年同期翻倍以上、季
增20%。資料中心營收單季季增21%至1,076
億美元，大規模雲端服務商貢獻600億。
► 董事會表示黃仁勳已辭職不會復任，交接存
在不確定性，對公司潛在影響：1)輝達動用
自身強勁獲利能力，支持AI業界，市場質疑
輝達收入持續性，接替者出現前，融資策略
可能偏保守；2) 事 件 引 發 加 強 出 口 管 制 憂
慮，新管理層或缺乏在政府遊說的能力，收
緊出口可能衝擊向亞洲出貨，拖累獲利。
► 公司EPS年增翻倍，毛利率維持75%，但預
估本益比從2024年40倍持續回落至現時不足
11倍，輝達若長期高速增長，市場應給予更
高估值。管理層變動令評價更低，但事件曝
光前，預估本益比已跌至約15倍。輝達AI訓

練與推理有領導地位，產能鎖定具優勢，但
市占率正流失予客製化晶片，疊加民主黨偏
向放慢AI發展，可能令未來晶片需求放慢。
輝達財報具韌性，交接存不確定性，市場早已不願提高評價

► Claude AI模型開發商Anthropic上市，IPO認
股價達83美元高於先前目標區間，加上私募
260億美元，總募資資金逾1,000億美元超過
SpaceX上市紀錄，凱基投顧預估Anthropic
成長動能來自：(1)訂閱與用量制帶動營收至
2030年複合成長近4成；(2)儘管市場擔憂降
價問題，然算力需求仍緊且Anthropic第一層
級付費Token市佔明顯高於其他AI模型開發
商，(3)算力成本認列早於營收，預估2027年
首季為獲利相對低點，2026年經濟FCF就轉
正且後續將逐年增長。
► Anthropic企業用戶淨收入留存率(NRR)高達
9倍，展現對企業客戶強大吸引力，未來最大
營收來源為企業將更多工作交付AI執行，而
非更多人訂閱AI，然仍須留意近期科技擾動
形塑AI短期逆風，後續風險觀察企業AI預算
是 否 下 滑( 市場終端需求) 、 第一層級付費
Token市佔比重是否快速下滑(AI模型市場地
位鬆動)及實收均價年減幅是否連2年高於推
論成本(降價開始由利潤吸收)等負面因素。

投資焦點
► 聯準會會議記錄顯示所有官員認為景氣若按
預期態勢發展，後續仍有升息必要，部分官
員擔憂通膨較先前更加頑固，應採取較原先
更緊縮的升息步伐，有兩位官員亦表示若通
膨朝不利方向發展，可能需升息2碼，利率市
場持續定價反映更鷹派貨幣政策路徑。
► 財政赤字方面，民主黨掌握國會兩院將進入
分立政府，可能阻止共和黨推出更多支出法
案，強制性財政支出佔總支出6成且佔比將續
升，而強制性支出僅能以立法縮減，民主黨
國會席次未達三分之二，任何立法將受總統
否決權鉗制，美國財政紀律難見明顯好轉。
► 考量聯準會升息預期持續、美國政府債務累
積和利息支出成為財政最大隱憂，以及明年
新債務上限協商將形塑美國公債市場焦點，
另AI發債潮和川普政策不確定性持續，研判
上述美國公債多項結構利空短期難消散，待
聯準會最快明年上半年停止升息且利率見高
後，再對美國公債進行配置。
多數聯準會官員言論維持鷹派論調市場對聯準會明年升息3碼預期仍在
聯準會認證景氣穩，高利率環境恐持續至明年上半年

投資焦點投資級債
► 聯準會確認景氣態勢仍強，近月美國科技大
廠蘋果與微軟聯袂發債融資，受大額債券供
給與市場風險偏好降溫影響，推升美國投資
級債殖利率持續揚升，預期明年AI資本支出
持續，整體美國投資級企業發債量將維持歷
史相對高位，債市投資仍以中短天期投資級
債為布局首選，待明年上半年利率升至高位
後，再逐步增加中長天期債部位配置。
► 美國大型企業獲利體現高成長動能，今年來
投資級 債企 業財 務 槓桿持 穩且信 用利 差微
升，體現在每單位槓桿利差僅略為上行，考

量債信基本面仍佳有望限制利差擴大幅度；
建議佈局A級至BBB級公司債、並聚焦風險
調整後正利差者如銀行、非必需消費、公用
事業和通訊產業鎖住好息，亦可留意能源、
必需消費、公用事業和資本設備與科技債相
關性較低產業分散風險，科技投資級債雖債
信體質穩健且違約風險有限，然逢大額發債
潮不建議交易，僅以持有至到期為主。
財務槓桿持穩而利差微升，反映在債信指標上AI企業持續融資加劇信用市場擾動
企業發債將持續，留意利率上行時鎖定好債

### Page 9

9
股市
 黃仁勳相關事件恐逐步強化美國對AI晶片出口管制、AI資料中心建設進程或將受到州政府緊縮政策
而造成評價面壓力並增添股價波動。短期可留意防禦股以增強抗震力以抵禦市場震盪。長期而言，
AI從全面看好轉向選股，且大優於小，市場波瀾過後產業長期正向趨勢未變。當前則可布局財務健
全度較高的高品質價值股，以獲取穩定報酬並降低市場波動影響。
 歐、日股可分散配置，利率走升有利銀行股。此外，隨財政政策支持，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好轉向選股、且大優於小，市場波
瀾後長期正向趨勢未變，當前可布局財務
健全度較高的高品質價值股
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 美伊談判走向反覆未明，油價維持高檔震盪。CPI物價高於預期，強化市場對聯準會升息步伐預估，
聯準會官員立場維持較高利率抑制通膨發展。建議以中短天期債券投資為主，可逢利率走升時布局
進行鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，
包含金融、非必需消費、通訊及公用事業等產業。

債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、非必需消費、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 CPI通膨支撐升息預期，美元走勢偏強。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理
商品
 美伊談判走向反覆，油價一度回落但再度反彈，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期AI相關變數持續發酵，高品質價值股有望獲得青睞

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH 
EARNINGS AGGREGATES 
STOXX 600 Earnings Outlook provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

Analyst: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA                                         November 5, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 25.0% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.5%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 11.9% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 6.9%.

 212 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 58.0% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 257 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 64.2% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 9, 40 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through November 5, 212 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 58% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.2% above estimates, which is below the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the STOXX 600 fo r Q3 2026 is 25%. The STOXX 600 expects to see share-
weighted earnings of €164.4B in Q3 2026, compared to shar e-weighted earnings of €131.6B (based on the year-ago 
earnings of the current constituents) in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improv ement in earnings relative to Q3 2025. The Energy sector 
has the highest earnings growth rate for the quarter, wh ile the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (153 .4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €35.7B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-69.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

The estimated revenue growth rate for the STOXX 600 for Q3 2026 is 11.9%. The STOXX 600 is expected to earn 
revenues of €1581.6B in Q3 2026, compared to €1413.6B in Q3 2025.

Eight of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in revenues relative to Q3 2025. The Energy 
sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the quarter, while the Consumer Cyclicals has the weakest anticipated 
growth compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest revenue growth rate (39.4%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€302.7B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €217.2B in Q3 2025.

The Consumer Cyclicals sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-1.2%) of any sector. It is expected to generate 
sales of €196.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €199.1B in Q3 2025.

## Document 429: Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade

Source PDF: all report pdf/Trade survives, but the battle for control intensifies_ 3 calls for global trade.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Global trade will remain resilient in 2027, but the focus will
shift from tariﬀs to ownership, technology and investment.
While not our base case, an escalation in an EU-China trade
war would rank among the most signiﬁcant risks to global
trade

Inga Fechner and Carsten Brzeski
Europe is undoubtedly facing a second 'China shock' – only this time around,
it's largely negative
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Trade survives politics
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Global goods trade continues to weather geopolitical shocks.
Over the past few years, trade has proved far more resilient
than global headlines suggest. We expect that resilience to
continue into 2027. Disruptions in global shipping, shifting
tariﬀ regimes and recurring geopolitical tensions have
repeatedly altered trade routes without derailing trade itself.
Strong demand for AI-related investment and infrastructure
should continue to support trade ﬂows, particularly across

Asia, while selective US-China decoupling is likely to beneﬁt
third countries that attract production and investment. For
2027, we expect global goods trade to remain positive,
expanding about 3% year-on-year. In short, trade survives
politics in 2027 because companies can still adapt faster
than governments can restrict them.
Our risky call: Ownership matters more than
trade
Tariﬀs were only the ﬁrst step. By 2027, the real battleground shifts
to capital, technology and ownership. As companies invest behind

tariﬀ walls, governments stop merely screening new investment
and begin requiring companies to restructure, separate sensitive
operations or divest existing strategic assets.
A foreign-owned battery plant, semiconductor facility, or data
centre once supported for bringing local investment and jobs is
now treated as a national-security risk. Companies that once
navigated geopolitical tensions through diversiﬁcation ﬁnd
themselves forced to choose sides. The question is no longer who

exports the most, but who controls the productive capacity on
which economies depend. Trade would continue, but the corporate
structures that have kept it moving would start to fracture as
governments alter trade ﬂows. This could potentially cause global
goods trade growth to stall or even contract in 2027.
Our bold call: EU-China trade war

Included in the following bundle
The EU and China enter an outright trade conﬂict. Europe abandons

its largely open-market approach and increasingly applies
reciprocity principles to market access and investment. Chinese
ﬁrms investing in strategic sectors face mandatory 50:50 joint
ventures, local ownership requirements and growing restrictions on
acquisitions.
At the same time, the EU imposes broad tariﬀs on Chinese imports,
with rates reaching 50% in politically sensitive sectors such as
vehicles, chemicals, machinery and electronics. China retaliates

with measures of similar scale.
The consequences are signiﬁcant. China accounts for roughly 15%
of global goods trade, while the EU accounts for around 13.5%.
Together they represent almost 30% of world goods trade. With the
EU being China's largest trading partner in goods and China being
the EU's second-largest trading partner in goods, a full-scale trade
conﬂict would rank among the most consequential economic
conﬂicts of the modern era.

## Document 435: US MARKETS CALL_ A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market_ Also_ Our New S&P 500 Targets.

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market_ Also_ Our New S&P 500 Targets..pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market? Also: Our New S&P 500 Targets.
Date:    9 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market? Also: Our New S&P 500 Targets.

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: A Bull-
Market Correction Or A Tech-
Wreck Bear Market? Also: Our
New S&P 500 Targets.
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 21 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Anthony Maw / Unsplash

I. Another Correction In A Bull Market
On September 15, we lowered our year-end S&P 500 target from 8,400 to
7,900. We turned more cautious on the stock market and expected buying
opportunities after the midterm elections. We got more of a buying
opportunity than we bargained for, as the S&P 500 dropped from 7,585.73
on September 15 to 6,910.39 on Friday. It is down 11.6% since its October
6 record high of 7818.93 (chart). We expected a pullback. It has turned
into a correction. We don't think it will lead to a bear market, simply
because we don't see an impending recession.

### Page 2

The Nasdaq-100 has also corrected by 19.8% since October 5 (chart). So
it is on the edge of turning into a bear market. The S&P 500 and Nasdaq-
100 both fell below their 200-day moving averages last week. Their
comparable equal-weight indexes found support at their 200-day moving
averages, so far.
The S&P 500's correction has been led by a sharp drop in its forward P/E
(charts). It plunged to 16.4 on Friday to the lowest level since the bear
market of 2022. Much of the recent damage has been attributable to the

S&P 500 Information Technology sector, particularly the S&P 500
Semiconductors industry, where the forward P/E fell to 9.4 on Friday.
Semiconductors were hit by news last week that the US government was
tightening export controls on semiconductors in response to allegations
that Nvidia and other US chip companies were indirectly selling their AI
chips to China or providing chips to other Asian data centers that the
Chinese accessed remotely. The fear is that tighter export controls will

lower the demand for chips and depress their prices. If so, then the drop in
the industry's valuation multiple will soon be followed by a slowdown in
earnings growth or even an outright drop in earnings.

### Page 3

To project the outlook for the S&P 500 in any year, we have been
multiplying our year-ahead S&P 500 EPS forecasts by a forward P/E
range of 17.0-20.0 (charts). The forward P/E and the index fell below the
bottom of our 2026 range last week. We haven't given up on a Santa
Claus rally, but are lowering our year-end S&P 500 target to 7,500. That
would be up 9.6% for the year from 6,845.50 at the end of last year.
Our S&P 500 range for next year is 7,650 to 9,000 (chart). We expect the

index to end 2027 between 8,500 and 9,000. We are sticking with our
Roaring 2020s forecast of 10,000 by 2029.

### Page 4

Maybe Elon can figure out how to buy Nvidia and hire Jensen to run it.
Nvidia is selling at just 8.1 forward earnings, as though it is still just a
gaming company (chart). Then again, Elon would have to find $3.02 trillion
to do the deal. That would be as crazy as putting people on Mars.
II. Performance
The latest Tech Wreck has been led by the semiconductor industry (chart).
It has also weighed on the MAGS ETF, which is now down 6.5% ytd
(chart). The XMAGS ETF is up by 5.4%. The S&P 500 is up just 0.9%.

### Page 5

Momentum ETFs have also been hard hit (chart).
III. Earnings
Q3's earnings data are now available for the S&P 500 (chart). Earnings
per share edged down from Q2's record results, which were boosted by
substantial mark-to-market investment gains for Alphabet and Amazon.
Revenue per share rose to a record high. The profit margin edged down to
16.3% from Q2's record high.
The industry analysts' consensus earnings estimates for 2027 and 2028
rose to record highs of $427.60 and $498.44 per share last week (chart).

### Page 6

Forward earnings rose to a record high of $421.84. It will converge with
the 2027 estimate at the end of this year. The forward P/E at the end of
last week was 16.4.
The Q3 growth rate of EPS was 27.1%, while Q4 is expected to be up
30.0% (chart).
IV. Valuation
The S&P 500 Growth forward P/E has plunged from 29.7 late last year to
17.8 now (chart). The S&P 500 Value valuation multiple has held up better
this year and is 17.3 currently.

### Page 7

The ratio of Growth's forward P/E to Value's is down to 1.03, the lowest
since the recession during the Great Financial Crisis (chart). It last peaked
in July 2024 at 1.84. The unwinding of the AI trade this year has
eliminated Growth's valuation premium over Value. That premium should
increase again once the AI selling panic subsides.
V. Sentiment
Contrarians can take some comfort from the spikes in the S&P 500 VIX
and the Nasdaq VIX to 35.9 and 45.3 on Friday (charts). With no

recession in sight, we doubt that these "fear gauges" will rise much from
here.

### Page 8

The two Bull/Bear Ratios we track are well below their long-term averages
(chart). We expect them to be even lower over the coming week. These
are also useful contrarian indicators.
V. Credit
The 10-year US Treasury bond yield fell to 5.04% last week in response to
the stock market rout (chart).
Interestingly, forward inflation-linked swaps have been mostly declining in
recent days as the stock market has weakened (chart).

### Page 9

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

車商獲利
美國傳統車商迎來皮卡改款週期，獲利表現有望提升
美國傳統車廠近年轉向生產更多高價卡車和SUV，因部分SUV和皮卡的調整後息稅前利潤率高達20%、超
過其他車型，也使美國新車平均交易價格在9月超過5萬美元。福特與通用汽車即將在2026年年底至2027
年迎來全尺寸皮卡改款與更新計畫，由於在推出新款或新一代卡車後，通常會有2~3年較佳的銷售期，預
估福特和通用汽車在2027年及之後的獲利有機會持續提升。
美國政策轉為支持燃油車發展，但高油價使油電、電動車需求提升

儘管美國政策轉向支持燃油車發展，但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下，使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔率
暴衝至16%，包括豐田、現代等品牌在美國的市占率持續上升。美國3Q26電動車銷量約佔新車總銷量
6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩腳步。由於油價上升使全球對電動車需求
增加，特斯拉2Q26、3Q26交車量也優於市場預期。
自駕車接受度提升，軟體營收增加帶動車商獲利
美國消費者對自動駕駛的認識與接受度正持續提升，而各車商正積極擴大自駕與軟體訂閱服務布局。通用
汽車預估其OnStar和Super Cruise自駕輔助營收在2026年將突破30億美元，福特汽車的軟體付費訂閱用
戶已達160萬戶。3Q26特斯拉FSD活躍用戶數已達174萬、年增67%，且北美新車FSD採用率超過75%。
相較傳統車商低於10%的硬體銷售利潤率，軟體毛利率高達50~70%，且訂閱制使車商部分營收轉為持續
性收入，將帶動其估值表現。

增加，但同時銷售量年減4%也顯
示市場需求正面臨更大挑戰。

車價等負面影響，但高收入族群
支撐使美國車市仍具韌性。然而
隨著利率上升、對消費者可負擔
性的懷疑日益增加，市場目前正
逐步將2026年美國輕型車銷售量
預估由1,610萬輛下修至1,560至
1,600萬輛區間。

檻轉趨嚴格，使消費者對新車的
需求下降。

高油價使美國電動車銷售逐步回溫

需求使油電、電動車並存
康寧儘管政策轉向支持燃油車發展，
但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下，
使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔

而儘管美國3Q26電動車銷量較去
年同期下降(3Q25為電動車補助
期限，基期較高)，但仍較2Q26
成長近8%，約佔美國新車總銷量
的6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯
示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩
腳步。以市場反應來看，美國車
市正從先前單一電動化路線，轉
向燃油、油電、電動車並存局面。
美國國會在2025年9/30 正式取
消購買或租賃新電動車的7,500
美元稅收抵免，造成美國電動車
銷售在4Q25開始大幅下滑。另
外，川普政府在今年9月底正式
宣布放寬美國企業燃油效率標準，
將未來新車的燃油效率年均提升
目標下調，使部分車商開始降低
電動車生產以減少虧損。
傳統車企如福特、通用汽車的電
動車部門EBIT利潤率可能介於-
10~-30%以下，燃油效率標準放
寬使其生產方向轉回燃油車發展，
也有助於改善整體獲利結構。

1
《銀行獲利穩增長，利收動能接棒領航，資本市場
短暫休整》
大型銀行3Q26獲利成長延續，但幅度收斂，主要
是非利息收入表現開始降溫，多數銀行認為投行案
源充足，需求只是遞延而非消失，債權相關業績因
利率走高而下滑，但股權相關業績仍有收獲；相比
之下，3Q獲利貢獻焦點一致轉向了淨利息收入，
業績增長得益於貸款與存款穩定成長，而財富管理
業務也提供了顯著的成長。部份銀行非利息費用成
長幅度大於收入成長幅度，導致效率比率變差。儘
管信貸品質仍健康無虞，但仍有部份銀行對潛在風
險展現謹慎態度，因而提高壞帳準備金。
展望後市，因淨利息收入的前景相對清晰，故可見

美大型銀行因此維持或上調2026全年淨利息收入
預期，或上修2026年股東權益報酬率，並重申或
下修壞帳率。相比之下，資本市場業務則需觀望，

除了活躍度已下滑外，交易收入在4Q時也易出現季
節性回落壓力。但各家管理層對經濟具韌性有相同
的共識，表示企業資本支出意願與消費信貸需求並
未因高利率環境而明顯轉弱，即便假設聯準會2027
年上半年之前升息3次，2027年淨利息收入仍具進
一步增長空間。
《摩根大通：企業投資與消費穩健，高利率紅利延
續》
3Q26營收年增8%、每股盈餘年增10%。淨利息收
入年增13%，受存款、信用卡及企業貸款成長帶動；
非利息收入年增3%；市場收入年增15%，由股票交
易大增帶動，固收業務下滑；投行業務年增15%，
主要受到諮詢費收入成長所帶動；非利息費用支出
年增12%，效率比率變差，呆帳與準備金皆較去年
同期減少。公司上調2026年淨利息收入預估，並預

期2027年進一步成長，反映資產負債表擴張及高利
率環境利多。管理層認為美國消費與企業投資仍具
韌性，惟通膨、升息、財政赤字及地緣政治風險仍
是後續觀察重點。
《花旗：上調2026利收預期、下調壞帳率、對潛在
風險謹慎》
3Q26營收年增8%、每股盈餘年增53%。淨利息收
入成長14%，受惠服務、財富管理、市場及信用卡
業務擴張；非利息收入下滑4%；市場收入受益於股
票收入成長；投資銀行收入受益於股權資本市場，
部份被併購諮詢與債權資本市場下滑所抵消；公司
增加壞帳準備金反映了管理潛在經濟風險的謹慎態
度；公司預期第四季市場收入將出現典型的季節性
下滑。公司上調2026年淨利息收入與股東權益報酬
率預期，下修壞帳率。管理層看好美國消費與企業
活動韌性，認為高利率環境及AI投資需求可持續支
撐成長。
《高盛：AI投資延續支撐成長，資本市場韌性猶存》
3Q26營收年增23%、每股盈餘年增39%。獲利成
長主要來自全球銀行與市場業務，股票交易收入大
增72%，固定收益、貨幣及大宗商品(FICC)則表現

平平，投資銀行收入成長21%，資產與財富管理收
入年增14%。管理層指出，美國經濟、勞動市場及

ASIA
MONTHLY 2026 年 11 月（No.308）
JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
1
保険業界の規制改革とインド経済
インド政府は、過去四半世紀にわたり国民生活の安定と金融仲介機能の強化に向けて保険市場の自由化を
推進してきたが、ここにきて消費者保護強化に向けた規制強化にかじを切ろうとしている。
■規制緩
和を受けてインドの保険市場は急成長
インド政府は、国民生活の安定と金融仲介機
能の強化に向けて、保険市場の発展を推進して
きた。この背景としては、以下の 2 点を指摘で
きる。
第 1 に、インドでは社会保障制度が整備途上
にある。そのため、予期せぬ疾病や事故をきっ
かけとする世帯所得の急減リスクを抑制するう
えで、医療保険や生命保険への加入が極めて重
要なセーフティネットとなる。
第 2 に、持続的なインド経済の発展のために
は、預入期間の短い銀行預金とは別に、インフ
ラ整備や産業育成の原動力となる長期かつ安定
的な国内資本の蓄積が必要である。保険会社が

集めて運用する巨額の資金は、まさにこの長期
資金供給の担い手として期待されてきた。
これらの目的の達成に向け、インド政府は過
去四半世紀にわたって段階的な規制緩和を進め
てきた（右上表）。2000 年の民間企業や外資企
業の参入自由化を皮切りに、2002 年のバンカシ
ュアランス（銀行窓口での保険販売）解禁、外
資出資比率上限の引き上げなどを進めた。
こうした一連の規制緩和によって多数の民
間・外国企業が参入し、市場競争が活性化した。
日本からも日本生命や東京海上グループを含め、
複数の保険会社が参入し、対印投資を牽引した。
市場参入や販売に関わる規制緩和に加え、中
間所得層の拡大、家計における金融リテラシー
の向上、そして保険サービスのデジタル化も相
まって、インドの保険市場は飛躍的な拡大を遂
げた。保険料収入は経済成長率を上回るペースで拡大し、近年の保険会社の運用資産残高は GDP
の約 4 分の 1 に達している（右下図）。
インド政府は、独立 100 周年を迎える 2047 年までに全国民が自らのリスクに適した保険に手

ごろな価格でアクセスできる「Insurance for All by 2047」の実現を目標に掲げており、今後もイ
ンドの保険市場は基本的に底堅い成長をたどると見込まれる。
上席主任研究員 熊谷 章太郎
(kumagai.shotaro@jri.co.jp）

時期 出来事
IRDAI（保険規制開発庁）発足
地場⺠間企業や外資の参⼊受付開始（外資の
出資上限は26％）
2002年 バンカシュアランス（銀⾏や銀⾏の所有する販売経 路を通じた保険販売）解禁
2015年 外資の出資上限を 26% から 49% へ引き上げ
2016年
新たな保険商品を販売する際の手続きにおける事
後届出制の導⼊開始（従来は事前承認制を採
用）
2021年 外資の出資上限を49% から74% へ引き上げ

2022年 オンラインおよび多様な流通チャネルに関する保険 販売規制の合理化
2023年 保険会社が代理店や中間業者に⽀払う手数料の 上限を撤廃
2025年 外資の出資上限を74％から100% へ引き上げ
2026年 IRDAI、保険販売制度の⼤規模改⾰に向けた公 開協議文書を公表
（資料）IRDAI、各種報道を基に日本総研作成
2000年
＜インドの保険業界に関する規制動向＞
